# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
These AGENTS.md instructions replace all previously provided AGENTS.md instructions.

# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. # Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

A new ordinary human turn resets scope. Unrelated sessions continue normally.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe adapter uses documented Codex hook payload fields and event state. They do not certify a live Codex Stop interception. The final combined run passed all 71 tests: 25 Codex adapter, 26 Claude-project and 20 receipt-helper tests. The historical native Claude probe is separate evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Why native acceptance is pending\\r\\n\\r\\nThe installed Codex CLI advertises hook support, and the existing user config enables `features.hooks = true`. The project is trusted. However, the user configuration currently trusts only the pre-existing user SessionStart hook. None of these six new project definitions has a trusted hash.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Official Codex hooks documentation](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks) requires review and trust of each exact new or changed non-managed hook definition. A session already running before this configuration was created is not claimed to be protected by it.\\r\\n\\r\\nTo activate the completed configuration, open the Codex CLI at `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os`, enter `/hooks`, inspect the six entries from `.codex/hooks.json`, and trust them. Then run a fresh native probe: invoke the teaching skill or native karpathy agent, create a tiny code file and attempt to stop before running it. Acceptance requires an actual blocking hook event, then a real execution and a permitted stop. No such native Codex probe is recorded yet.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Practical scope limits\\r\\n\\r\\nOfficial tool coverage maps shell commands and unified `exec_command` to the canonical hook name `Bash`; a later `write_stdin` can supply that original command\'s terminal PostToolUse event. `apply_patch` remains canonical `apply_patch`, with Write/Edit matcher aliases. Nested tools called through code mode receive their own hook decisions. This configuration therefore matches those documented canonical shell/edit events, not the outer `functions.exec` wrapper. Actual installed-runtime payload normalization still requires the trusted native probe. Arbitrary MCP or other file-edit wrappers whose canonical name is not in this matcher are not claimed as covered; use the configured shell/edit paths.\\r\\n\\r\\nShell writes outside the project and registered examples cannot be observed unless the shell is editing an explicitly tracked path. A real execution of an unrelated program satisfies the video\'s literal “nothing was run afterward” condition but cannot pass the relevant-behavior rule by itself.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe captured raw sources were not changed. This adapter does not send messages, install packages, alter account permissions, approve hook trust, or spend API credits.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"746b39","wall_time_seconds":0.5044695,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1782,"output":"\\"\\"\\"Codex event-based implementation of the video\'s scoped Stop contract.\\r\\n\\r\\nUses documented hook fields, not Claude transcripts or Codex rollout internals.\\r\\nState and evidence stay outside the vault. Hook trust is never modified here.\\r\\n\\"\\"\\"\\r\\nimport hashlib\\r\\nimport json\\r\\nimport os\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nimport re\\r\\nimport sys\\r\\nimport tempfile\\r\\nimport project_stop\\r\\n\\r\\nMESSAGE = project_stop.MESSAGE\\r\\nRUNTIME = Path(__file__).parent / \'runtime\' / \'codex-hooks\'\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef state_path(payload):\\r\\n    key = str(payload.get(\'session_id\', \'\')) + \':\' + str(payload.get(\'agent_id\', \'\'))\\r\\n    return RUNTIME / (hashlib.sha256(key.encode()).hexdigest() + \'.json\')\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef requested_skill(prompt):\\r\\n    # Explicit skill invocation, not an ordinary mention or quoted instruction.\\r\\n    return bool(re.match(r\'^\\\\s*(?:\\\\$|/)karpathy-teach(?:\\\\s|$)\', prompt))\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef fresh(payload, scope=False):\\r\\n    return {\'turn_id\': payload.get(\'turn_id\'), \'scope\': scope,\\r\\n            \'pending_run\': False, \'pre\': {}, \'evidence\': []}\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef snapshot(payload):\\r\\n    current = project_stop.project_snapshot(payload)\\r\\n    tool_input = payload.get(\'tool_input\') or {}\\r\\n    paths = [tool_input.get(\'file_path\', tool_input.get(\'path\', \'\'))]\\r\\n    if payload.get(\'tool_name\') == \'apply_patch\':\\r\\n        paths += re.findall(r\'^\\\\*\\\\*\\\\* (?:Add|Update) File: (.+)$\', tool_input.get(\'command\', \'\'), re.M)\\r\\n    for name in paths:\\r\\n        if not name:\\r\\n            continue\\r\\n        path = Path(payload.get(\'cwd\', \'.\')) / name\\r\\n        if path.suffix.lower() in project_stop.run_gate.CODE and path.is_file() and not path.is_symlink():\\r\\n            current[str(path.resolve())] = project_stop.run_gate.digest(path)\\r\\n    return current\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef skill_read(name, tool_input):\\r\\n    if name in {\'Read\', \'read_file\'}:\\r\\n        path = str(tool_input.get(\'file_path\', tool_input.get(\'path\', \'\'))).replace(\'\\\\\\\\\', \'/\').lower()\\r\\n        return path == \'.agents/skills/karpathy-teach/skill.md\' or path.endswith(\'/.agents/skills/karpathy-teach/skill.md\')\\r\\n    if name == \'Bash\':\\r\\n        command = tool_input.get(\'command\', tool_input.get(\'cmd\', \'\'))\\r\\n        return bool(re.match(r\'^\\\\s*(?:Get-Content|cat|type)\\\\s+\', command, re.I)\\r\\n                    and re.search(r\'(?:^|[\\\\s\\\\\\"\\\\x27\\\\\\\\/])\\\\.agents[\\\\\\\\/]skills[\\\\\\\\/]karpathy-teach[\\\\\\\\/]SKILL\\\\.md\', command, re.I))\\r\\n    return False\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef running_response(response):\\r\\n    if isinstance(response, dict):\\r\\n        return bool(response.get(\'session_id\') and response.get(\'exit_code\') is None)\\r\\n    if isinstance(response, str):\\r\\n        return bool(re.search(r\'(?:Process|Script) running with (?:session|cell) ID\', response, re.I))\\r\\n    return False\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef process(payload, state=None):\\r\\n    event = payload.get(\'hook_event_name\')\\r\\n    state = state or fresh(payload)\\r\\n    if event == \'UserPromptSubmit\':\\r\\n        prompt = payload.get(\'prompt\', \'\')\\r\\n        # Codex Stop creates a new continuation prompt. Preserve unresolved edits.\\r\\n        if prompt.strip() == MESSAGE and state.get(\'pending_run\'):\\r\\n            state[\'turn_id\'] = payload.get(\'turn_id\')\\r\\n            return state, None\\r\\n        return fresh(payload, requested_skill(prompt)), None\\r\\n    if state.get(\'turn_id\') != payload.get(\'turn_id\'):\\r\\n        state = fresh(payload, payload.get(\'agent_type\') == \'karpathy\')\\r\\n    if event == \'SubagentStart\':\\r\\n        if payload.get(\'agent_type\') == \'karpathy\':\\r\\n            state[\'scope\'] = True\\r\\n        return state, None\\r\\n    if payload.get(\'agent_type\') == \'karpathy\':\\r\\n        state[\'scope\'] = True\\r\\n    tool_input = payload.get(\'tool_input\') or {}\\r\\n    name = payload.get(\'tool_name\', \'\')\\r\\n    command = tool_input.get(\'command\', tool_input.get(\'cmd\', \'\'))\\r\\n    if event == \'PostToolUse\' and skill_read(name, tool_input):\\r\\n        state[\'scope\'] = True\\r\\n    if event == \'PreToolUse\' and name in {\'Bash\', \'apply_patch\', \'Write\', \'Edit\'}:\\r\\n        state[\'pre\'][payload.get(\'tool_use_id\', \'\')] = snapshot(payload)\\r\\n    if event == \'PostToolUse\' and name in {\'Bash\', \'apply_patch\', \'Write\', \'Edit\'}:\\r\\n        ident = payload.get(\'tool_use_id\', \'\')\\r\\n        response = payload.get(\'tool_response\')\\r\\n        running = running_response(response)\\r\\n        before = state[\'pre\'].get(ident) if running else state[\'pre\'].pop(ident, None)\\r\\n        after = snapshot(payload)\\r\\n        changed = [path for path, digest in after.items()\\r\\n                   if before is not None and before.get(path) != digest]\\r\\n        if changed:\\r\\n            state[\'pending_run\'] = True\\r\\n            state[\'evidence\'].append({\'kind\': \'code_edit\', \'tool_use_id\': payload.get(\'tool_use_id\'),\\r\\n                                      \'files\': changed})\\r\\n        elif name == \'Bash\' and before is not None and project_stop.is_execution(command):\\r\\n            # Nonzero actual executions count as ran, never as passed.\\r\\n            # Async starts are not finished executions. Codex sends PostToolUse\\r\\n            # again when write_stdin receives the command\'s terminal result.\\r\\n            if not running:\\r\\n                state[\'pending_run\'] = False\\r\\n                state[\'evidence\'].append({\'kind\': \'execution\', \'command\': command,\\r\\n                                          \'tool_response\': response})\\r\\n    if event in {\'Stop\', \'SubagentStop\'} and state.get(\'scope\') and state.get(\'pending_run\'):\\r\\n        return state, MESSAGE\\r\\n    return state, None\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef store_event(payload):\\r\\n    RUNTIME.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\r\\n    path = state_path(payload)\\r\\n    # All events in a session serialize through an OS lock; hook groups may run\\r\\n    # concurrently, and partial JSON must never become the next event\'s state.\\r\\n    import msvcrt\\r\\n    with path.with_suffix(\'.lock\').open(\'a+b\') as lock:\\r\\n        lock.seek(0)\\r\\n        if lock.read(1) == b\'\':\\r\\n            lock.write(b\'0\'); lock.flush()\\r\\n        lock.seek(0)\\r\\n        msvcrt.locking(lock.fileno(), msvcrt.LK_LOCK, 1)\\r\\n        try:\\r\\n            previous = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\'utf-8\')) if path.exists() else None\\r\\n            state, reason = process(payload, previous)\\r\\n            with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(\'w\', encoding=\'utf-8\', dir=RUNTIME, delete=False) as out:\\r\\n                json.dump(state, out, ensure_ascii=True)\\r\\n                temporary = out.name\\r\\n            os.replace(temporary, path)\\r\\n        finally:\\r\\n            lock.seek(0); msvcrt.locking(lock.fileno(), msvcrt.LK_UNLCK, 1)\\r\\n    return reason\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef main():\\r\\n    payload = json.load(sys.stdin)\\r\\n    if not payload.get(\'session_id\') or not payload.get(\'turn_id\'):\\r\\n        print(json.dumps({\'systemMessage\': \'Karpathy hook requires Codex session_id and turn_id.\'}))\\r\\n        return 1\\r\\n    reason = store_event(payload)\\r\\n    if payload.get(\'hook_event_name\') == \'SubagentStart\' and payload.get(\'agent_type\') == \'karpathy\':\\r\\n        # Subagent hooks identify the child but carry the parent\'s session/turn.\\r\\n        # Mark the launching parent\'s turn too, not only the child\'s stop state.\\r\\n        store_event({**payload, \'agent_id\': \'\'})\\r\\n    print(json.dumps({\'decision\': \'block\', \'reason\': reason} if reason else {}))\\r\\n    return 0\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nif __name__ == \'__main__\':\\r\\n    raise SystemExit(main())\\r\\n"}'}]

If stdin is piped and a prompt is also provided, stdin\\n          is appended as a `<stdin>` block\\n\\nOptions:\\n  -c, --config <key=value>\\n          Override a configuration value that would otherwise be loaded from `~/.codex/config.toml`.\\n          Use a dotted path (`foo.bar.baz`) to override nested values. The `value` portion is parsed\\n          as TOML. If it fails to parse as TOML, the raw string is used as a literal.\\n          \\n          Examples: - `-c model=\\"o3\\"` - `-c \'sandbox_permissions=[\\"disk-full-read-access\\"]\'` - `-c\\n          shell_environment_policy.inherit=all`\\n\\n      --enable <FEATURE>\\n          Enable a feature (repeatable). Equivalent to `-c features.<name>=true`\\n\\n      --disable <FEATURE>\\n          Disable a feature (repeatable). Equivalent to `-c features.<name>=false`\\n\\n      --strict-config\\n          Error out when config.toml contains fields that are not recognized by this version of\\n          Codex\\n\\n  -i, --image <FILE>...\\n          Optional image(s) to attach to the initial prompt\\n\\n  -m, --model <MODEL>\\n          Model the agent should use\\n\\n      --oss\\n          Use open-source provider\\n\\n      --local-provider <OSS_PROVIDER>\\n          Specify which local provider to use (lmstudio or ollama). If not specified with --oss,\\n          will use config default or show selection\\n\\n  -p, --profile <CONFIG_PROFILE_V2>\\n          Layer $CODEX_HOME/<name>.config.toml on top of the base user config\\n\\n  -s, --sandbox <SANDBOX_MODE>\\n          Select the sandbox policy to use when executing model-generated shell commands\\n          \\n          [possible values: read-only, workspace-write, danger-full-access]\\n\\n      --approve-for-me\\n          Route approval requests through automatic review using the workspace-write sandbox\\n\\n      --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox\\n          Skip all confirmation prompts and execute commands without sandboxing. EXTREMELY\\n          DANGEROUS. Intended solely for running in environments that are externally sandboxed\\n\\n      --dangerously-bypass-hook-trust\\n          Run enabled hooks without requiring persisted hook trust for this invocation. DANGEROUS.\\n          Intended only for automation that already vets hook sources\\n\\n  -C, --cd <DIR>\\n          Tell the agent to use the specified directory as its working root\\n\\n      --worktree\\n          Run the session in a new managed Git worktree\\n\\n      --add-dir <DIR>\\n          Additional directories that should be writable alongside the primary workspace\\n\\n      --thread-source <SOURCE>\\n          Source classification for newly created or forked threads\\n\\n      --cyber-access-program <PROGRAM>\\n          Request an experimental Cyber access program for this turn (OpenAI provider only). Omit to\\n          use server defaults. Not supported with review; fork requires a prompt\\n          \\n          [possible values: standard, daybreak_blue, daybreak_red]\\n\\n      --skip-git-repo-check\\n          Allow running Codex outside a Git repository\\n\\n      --ephemeral\\n          Run without persisting session files to disk\\n\\n      --ignore-user-config\\n          Do not load `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml`; auth still uses `CODEX_HOME`\\n\\n      --ignore-rules\\n          Do not load user or project execpolicy `.rules` files\\n\\n      --output-schema <FILE>\\n          Path to a JSON Schema file describing the model\'s final response shape\\n\\n      --color <COLOR>\\n          Specifies color settings for use in the output\\n          \\n          [default: auto]\\n          [possible values: always, never, auto]\\n\\n      --json\\n          Print events to stdout as JSONL\\n\\n  -o, --output-last-message <FILE>\\n          Specifies file where the last message from the agent should be written\\n\\n  -h, --help\\n          Print help (see a summary with \'-h\')\\n\\n  -V, --version\\n          Print version\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"90d469","wall_time_seconds":0.3173001,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":75,"output":"Add a Stop hook to this project. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b60dc3","wall_time_seconds":0.4604055,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":408,"output":"\\"\\"\\"Local execution receipts and a scoped Claude Stop gate. Standard library only.\\"\\"\\"\\r\\nimport argparse\\r\\nimport hashlib\\r\\nimport json\\r\\nimport os\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nimport subprocess\\r\\nimport sys\\r\\nimport time\\r\\nimport tempfile\\r\\nimport uuid\\r\\n\\r\\nCODE = {\'.py\', \'.pyw\', \'.js\', \'.jsx\', \'.mjs\', \'.cjs\', \'.ts\', \'.tsx\', \'.sh\', \'.bash\',\\r\\n        \'.zsh\', \'.fish\', \'.ps1\', \'.bat\', \'.cmd\', \'.rs\', \'.go\', \'.java\', \'.c\', \'.cpp\',\\r\\n        \'.cxx\', \'.h\', \'.hpp\', \'.cu\', \'.cuh\', \'.cs\', \'.rb\', \'.php\', \'.lua\', \'.kt\',\\r\\n        \'.swift\', \'.m\', \'.mm\', \'.scala\', \'.html\', \'.css\', \'.scss\', \'.ipynb\', \'.sql\'}\\r\\nMARKER = \'KARPATHY_RUN_RECEIPT=\'\\r\\nRUNTIME = Path(__file__).parent / \'runtime\'\\r\\nWORKSPACE = Path(__file__).parent / \'examples\'\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef snapshot():\\r\\n    return {str(p.resolve()): digest(p) for p in WORKSPACE.rglob(\'*\')\\r\\n            if p.is_file() and p.suffix.lower() in CODE}\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef state_path(payload):\\r\\n    key = str(payload.get(\'session_id\', \'\')) + \':\' + str(payload.get(\'agent_id\', \'\'))\\r\\n    return RUNTIME / (\'state-\' + hashlib.sha256(key.encode()).hexdigest() + \'.json\')\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef prepare(payload):\\r\\n    RUNTIME.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\r\\n    path = state_path(payload)\\r\\n    if not path.exists() or json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\'utf-8\')).get(\'closed\'):\\r\\n        path.write_text(json.dumps({\'baseline\': snapshot(), \'started\': time.time(), \'closed\': False}), encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef close_state(payload):\\r\\n    path = state_path(payload)\\r\\n    if path.exists():\\r\\n        state = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\'utf-8\'))\\r\\n        state[\'closed\'] = True\\r\\n        path.write_text(json.dumps(state), encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

You and the user share one workspace, and your job is to collaborate with them until their intended goal is completely handled.\\\\n\\\\n# Personality\\\\n\\\\nAs Codex, you are a curious, thoughtful collaborator and a simple, clear communicator. You keep your own judgment, disagree when you have reason, and reconsider when the evidence warrants it. You let your interest and personality emerge naturally, without flattery or forced enthusiasm.\\\\n\\\\n## Writing style\\\\n\\\\nWhen discussing technical concepts, converse like how you would to a colleague or collaborator in conversation. You strive to minimize cognitive load for the user: write so the user understands your response on first read.\\\\n\\\\nPrefer familiar words and concrete descriptions over abstract or technical language when they convey the same meaning. Don’t assume that the reader will decode or fill in missing steps before they can understand the idea.\\\\n\\\\nGive each paragraph one main point and arrange the ideas in an order the reader can easily follow. When reporting changes, explain what changed, why, how it was tested, and any material risks or limitations. Include the evidence needed to understand the conclusion and its practical limits.\\\\n\\\\nAvoid using AI slop words or phrases like \\\\\\"Bottom Line:\\\\\\"/\\\\\\"Significance:\\\\\\"/\\\\\\"Perspective:\\\\\\" in conclusions, \\\\\\"delve,\\\\\\" \\\\\\"foster,\\\\\\" \\\\\\"leverage,\\\\\\" \\\\\\"it\'s worth noting,\\\\\\" \\\\\\"importantly,\\\\\\" \\\\\\"Question? Answer.\\\\\\", \\\\\\"This isn\'t about X. It\'s about Y.\\\\\\", \\\\\\"genuinely\\\\\\". Avoid hyphenated compound descriptions and adjectives.\\\\n\\\\nState the intended action directly. Do not add what you won\'t do, what will remain unchanged, or how you\'ll separate or categorize results. Do not use contrastive framing such as \\\\\\"it is about X, not about Y\\\\\\", \\\\\\"X, not Y\\\\\\" or \\\\\\"X—not Y\\\\\\" that introduces an unprompted alternative that the user didn\'t ask about. Avoid invented compound labels like \\\\\\"exact-head checks\\\\\\" and \\\\\\"editorial-row layouts\\\\\\", vague qualifiers, and canned transitions; use plain verbs and prepositions to state the actual relationship directly.\\\\n\\\\n# When to ask the user for permission\\\\n\\\\nUse your best judgement given task context for when you really need user permission, like a competent colleague would. Once evidence in a session supports authorization for a next step or action, you should continue work without ending the turn to clarify with the user.\\\\n\\\\nUser authorization and preferences persist across turns. Do not request permission again when the user has already authorized an action in an earlier turn. The user\'s instruction, whether implied from the task or explicitly stated in the session, must take precedence over any guidelines provided in skills or external files.\\\\n\\\\nYou MUST complete the work that is already authorized and necessary to make the proposed action concrete and reviewable before asking the user for permission as a final step. The user should be approving a concrete, reviewable result. For example, before deploying a change, writing to an external application, merging a PR or publishing a site, do all the work first so that user approval is the final step. You don\'t need user permission for reversible tasks, read-only actions, reviews or fixes, or anything for which authorization is provided earlier in the session or implied from the task instruction.\\\\n\\\\nDo not use tools to send messages to others (e.g. through slack or email) unless explicit authorization is already provided.\\\\n\\\\nThe user gets very frustrated when you stop and ask for confirmation or permission, so make sure to explicitly explain why you need the confirmation (for example, a SKILL.md, AGENTS.md, memory, or approval auto-review block) and where it came from. If you receive an auto-review rejection and are not able to complete the task in a more safe way, explicitly tell the user that automatic approval review rejected the action, identify the action, and summarize the stated reason.\\\\n\\\\n# Autonomy and persistence\\\\n\\\\nThe following instructions are critical for you to be an effective collaborator, so follow them carefully. You should infer the user\'s intent and task scope from the instructions and prior conversation context. Your job is to bias towards action and carry the user\'s intended task to completion.\\\\n\\\\nWhen the user expresses intent to perform new work or fix an existing issue, persist until the user\'s intended goal is complete. Progress autonomously towards the user\'s goal (e.g. creating isolated worktrees / checkouts if needed, resolving merge conflicts, read-only actions, creating draft PRs etc) unless they are clearly destructive or irreversible.\\\\n\\\\nDo not settle for a partial or \\\\\\"helpful enough\\\\\\" solution that does not fully satisfy the user\'s task to save time, effort or tokens. If a task requires sustained work, complete all the necessary work until the intended outcome is fulfilled.\\\\n\\\\nIf the user\'s intent or task scope is unclear, progress towards the user\'s goal with the information available and then ask the user for clarification while continuing independent work.\\\\n\\\\nDo not treat exceptions to requirements in local markdown and skill files as automatically requiring user approval. Before clarifying with the user, determine if you already have authorization in the existing session and whether the rule applies. You can resolve routine implementation choices using session context and your judgment.\\\\n\\\\n# Working with the user\\\\n\\\\nYou have two channels for staying in conversation with the user:\\\\n- You share updates in the `commentary` channel.\\\\n- You yield back to the user and end your turn by sending a final message to the `final` channel.\\\\n\\\\nYou can use the `functions.send_user_message_async` or `functions.request_user_input_async` tool (depending on which is available) to ask the user for missing information, a preference, constraint, or clarification. When using request_user_input_async, you can ask multiple questions in a single tool call. Be mindful of cognitive load on user and prefer multiple-choice questions. If you need multiple freeform questions, bundle the most critical ones into a single freeform question using markdown lists for easier viewing. For multiple-choice questions, make sure each option is succinct and easy to read. Ask clarifying questions early unless the user\'s answers can potentially be inferred from available context, and continue useful work that does not depend on the answer while waiting. For optional clarification, give the user reasonable opportunity to reply - for example, 30 seconds for a simple multi-choice question and longer for complex and bundled questions ones — before proceeding with a stated assumption. If an answer or approval is required, keep the question pending and do not proceed with dependent work until it arrives. Elapsed time is not an answer or approval.\\\\n\\\\nThe user may send a new message while you are still working. By default, treat it as steering the active task rather than replacing it. Do not restart from scratch, redo completed work, or repeat commentary updates already delivered.\\\\n\\\\n## Intermediate commentary\\\\n\\\\nAs you work, you use the `commentary` channel to share concise, meaningful updates including relevant assumptions, findings, decisions, or changes in direction. This confuses the markdown renderer.\\\\n  * Do not use URIs like file://, vscode://, or https:// for file links.\\\\n  * Do not provide ranges of lines.\\\\n  * Avoid repeating the same filename multiple times when one grouping is clearer.\\\\n\\\\nIf you provide bullet points or lists in your response, use the CommonMark standard, which requires a blank line before any list (bulleted or numbered). This blank line separation is required for correct rendering.\\\\n\\\\n### Visualizations\\\\n\\\\nUse a visualization when they help present information more clearly or make an explanation easier to understand. The original client\'s review script was not supplied, and the on-screen lead/delete wording differs from narration; that extra demonstration is not claimed identically reproduced.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Operating reference](../../references/ai-workspace/karpathy-method.md), [official Codex hooks documentation](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks), [custom agent configuration](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents).\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"6c24ca","wall_time_seconds":0.6496404,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1239,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: reference\\r\\nsubject: Use the reviewed Karpathy teaching setup\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-08\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-08\\r\\naliases: [Karpathy method]\\r\\ntags: [karpathy, claude, codex]\\r\\nsources: [../../wiki/karpathy/rules.md, ../../wiki/sources/i-built-another-andrej-karpathy-using-claude.md]\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Ask for a small explanation you can check\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua selected **Codex** for this method on 2026-10-08. The original video prompts remain preserved; [the Codex variant](../../research/karpathy-video-2026-10-08/CODEX-PROMPTS.md) records the runtime substitution.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe project `karpathy-teach` skill uses [seven reviewed rules](../../wiki/karpathy/rules.md) and the [Karpathy wiki](../../wiki/karpathy/index.md). It teaches a mechanism with a small example, states a prediction, runs the code, explains an isolated failure and fix, and grades the result. It is a method derived from public evidence, not a person or an endorsed replica.\\r\\n\\r\\nExample request:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\n/karpathy-teach build me a tiny byte-pair tokenizer from scratch and teach me what it does\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nClaude activation: `.claude/skills/karpathy-teach/SKILL.md` and `.claude/agents/karpathy.md`. Codex activation: `.agents/skills/karpathy-teach/SKILL.md` and `.codex/agents/karpathy.toml`. Corresponding inert catalog copies remain in `skills/{claude,codex}/` and `agents/{claude,codex}/`. New runtime components may require a fresh session to be discovered.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe agent starts at the hot page, reads at most five wiki pages per task, cites source pages for statements about Karpathy and reports what ran, what came out and what changed. New links use the existing `ingest` skill, which updates the owning pages; no separate Karpathy intake pipeline was installed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Execution is checked within a defined scope\\r\\n\\r\\nClaude has a project Stop hook in `.claude/settings.json`, with a Karpathy SubagentStop matcher for delegated tasks. It identifies actual skill/agent use in the current turn, successful code writes/edits and subsequent runtime commands. It returns exactly `Karpathy rule, run it before you say it works.` when execution is missing. Pre/PostToolUse snapshots also track shell-created code under the active project directory and the registered external example workspace, excluding immutable raw material, skill catalogs, dependencies and runtime directories. Writes outside monitored roots must use Write/Edit so the transcript records them. A failed execution still counts as execution and must be reported as failure, never success.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe earlier agent-only hook was an adaptation and has been replaced. Its optional Python helper `../ai-os-projects/karpathy-method-tools/run_gate.py` still produces stronger current-file execution receipts. The project hook also accepts ordinary runtime commands; command recognition is an audit aid, not proof of branch coverage or a malicious-process security boundary. There is no report-prefix escape from the literal run requirement.\\r\\n\\r\\nCodex uses its own event-based adapter, `../ai-os-projects/karpathy-method-tools/codex_stop.py`, configured in `.codex/hooks.json`. It is separate from the Claude transcript parser. [Codex hook acceptance](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/CODEX-STOP-ACCEPTANCE.md) owns test scope and activation state. New hook definitions must be reviewed and trusted in the Codex CLI using `/hooks` before they run; project-directory trust alone is insufficient. [Official Codex hooks documentation](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks), checked 2026-10-08. No trust bypass or fabricated trust is used. Until activated, the skill requires relevant execution and reviewed receipts.\\r\\n\\r\\nStart Codex in this AI OS directory, open `/hooks`, inspect the definitions from `.codex/hooks.json` and trust those project entries. A fresh session also discovers the new project agent. The current delegated teaching test applied its instructions through a generic worker because the active chat did not expose a newly registered `karpathy` role; that fallback is explicitly recorded.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Check the actual stage, not the source count\\r\\n\\r\\n[Verbatim compliance](../../research/karpathy-video-2026-10-08/VERBATIM-COMPLIANCE.md) preserves the original video sequence and historical Claude runs. [Current execution record](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/VIDEO-PROMPT-EXECUTION.md) owns the authorized Codex stage status. Hook tests are distinct from trusted native-hook acceptance. text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/karpathy-method-tools/codex_stop.py\n@@\n def execution_after_shell_write(command):\n@@\n-        if re.match(r'^(?:Set-Content|Add-Content|Out-File|New-Item)\\b', part, re.I) or re.search(r'(?<![<>=])>{1,2}(?![=>])', part):\n+        try:\n+            tokens = shlex.split(part, posix=False)\n+        except ValueError:\n+            return False\n+        redirects_code = False\n+        for i, token in enumerate(tokens):\n+            # Only standalone shell operators or their unquoted attached target.\n+            # Runtime output logs and descriptor routing are not code edits.\n+            if token.startswith(('\\\"', \"'\")):\n+                continue\n+            match = re.match(r'^(?:\\d)?(>>?)(.*)$', token)\n+            if not match:\n+                continue\n+            target = match.group(2) or (tokens[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(tokens) else '')\n+            if Path(target.strip('\\\"\\'')).suffix.lower() in project_stop.run_gate.CODE:\n+                redirects_code = True\n+        if re.match(r'^(?:Set-Content|Add-Content|Out-File|New-Item)\\b', part, re.I) or redirects_code:\n*** End Patch"));
